# GSC Work Queue — TODO.md (v57 · 2026-10-23)

**Purpose.** The single work sink for the project: every future-work item any session surfaces — re-tests, rebuilds, backfills, corporate actions, queue seeds — routes **here** (routing per MODEL §7.1, v4.2.0). The register's own open items — pending user decisions and register-integrity holds, cap 7 — live in the head block `▶ Open items (register)` below (MODEL v4.5.0). This file is *not* authoritative state: `_REGISTER.md` wins on state, `MODEL.md` on rules, memo appendices on per-name detail. Nothing here is a verdict or a recommendation. Framework ideas go to `queue/IDEAS.md`, not here.

**Ticker discipline.** Tickers are suggested keys for convenience; resolved and verified at run time per §7.1a (prior-check on company name + ISIN, never the key alone).

## ▶ IDEAS candidates (attended triage)

*Copied verbatim from routine row files by INTAKE; attended triage moves each to `queue/IDEAS.md` or strikes it. INTAKE never clears this block.*

The 51 candidates of 2026-10-04/05 were triaged at the MODEL v4.5.0 build (2026-10-06): each is placed BUILT, CLOSED or PARKED (or struck) in `queue/IDEAS.md` §T (v11), rewritten to the final state after Jakub's method decisions J1–J15 (2026-10-06). The four lines below came with INTAKE 2026-10-07 on `main` (register v308) and were carried in by the sync merge of 2026-10-07; they await attended triage. The fourteen Gate-0 candidates of 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-14 (6383.T, RSW.L, QCOM, BA.L, 8002.T, INTU, ANDR.VI, IMI.L, DPLM.L, SKF-B.ST, ROCK-B.CO, BNZL.L, REL.L, EXPN.L) were closed by MODEL v4.6.0 (2026-10-15) and moved to `queue/IDEAS.md` §G0 (v14).

- 2026-10-05 · LSS.PA ANALYSE — Pay-ratio-only disclosure: French/EU filers publish highest-paid ÷ median (CSRD S1-16) without either amount; a P17 note on when a published ratio plus a sourced national or group median may stand in (or must stay INDETERMINATE) would make these calls repeatable.
- 2026-10-05 · SPX.L ANALYSE — P4 "sustained decline" where returns fall in acquisition-led steps and then plateau (SPX.L: ROCE 22% → 11% over FY2016–25, FY2022–25 flat at 11–13%, FCFROCE rising since FY2022) — should §2·1A define "sustained" (e.g. consecutive years, or latest N years below all earlier window years)?
- 2026-10-06 · BABA REVIEW — Outside-director pay vs accounting expense (BABA v2): the P17 director test reads the disclosed total, which includes the share-based compensation expense recognised in the year. At Alibaba that expense almost doubled to RMB 4.63m per INED, which pushed six seats over the US stop. Cash fees alone are $211–396k. Should the test read the grant-date value of awards made in the year, or the expense, when a filer discloses both?
- 2026-10-06 · BABA REVIEW — Investment-heavy operating companies (BABA v2): equity-method stakes and long-term equity securities are about one-third of total assets and stay in CE under the subtractive rule (0700.HK precedent). Worst-of-four ROCE is 7.5% on that basis. Should the rule name a treatment, or a disclosed alternate read, when non-operating investments are this large but the substitution rule does not apply?
- 2026-10-07 · HLMA.L ANALYSE — Breadth silence rule for group units: when the filer gives only a group-level "no customer above 10%" statement, should that statement bound a unit's concentration (it caps any one customer at 10% ÷ the unit's revenue share) — HLMA.L 2026-10-07 read it as silence, flagged, then contained.
- 2026-10-07 · HLMA.L ANALYSE — Acquisition stop OI proxy: IFRS 3 full-year "profit after tax" (after tax and acquired-intangible amortisation) overstates pre-deal EV/OI by roughly 1.3–1.6×; consider a pre-tax gross-up rule for the proxy (HLMA.L 2026-10-07: 14.2–16.4× PAT read as above worth).
- 2026-10-08 · NOVO-B.CO REVIEW — Whole-company asset acquisitions inside "purchase of intangible assets" (Akero ≈ DKK 30bn, FY2025): the P20 constructed tier counts them as maintenance capex because they bring no revenue growth, which cuts the governing FCF and the trigger (here 170 → 57.5). Should a whole-company asset deal be read as a deal (§2·1C·3) rather than capex, or the year's FCF leg carry a named P28-style test?
- 2026-10-08 · NOVO-B.CO REVIEW — "Stock still above worth" in the ADVERSE limb when the year's FCF read is depressed by one-off capex (MCap/FCF 34× against EV/OI 9.6× and a 3-year FCF read of 15×): should the live-behaviour test name which read governs, rather than leave it to the in-doubt rule?
- 2026-10-08 · HFG.DE REVIEW — Loss-year P17 strike test ("the year total rose on the prior year's"): where only board aggregates are printed for the prior year, the floor/ceiling rule gives no determinate comparison of year totals; should the rule state how a rise is tested from aggregates (e.g. compare the current per-person total with the prior aggregate ÷ members floor and ceiling)?
- 2026-10-08 · EQNR REVIEW — Minority stakes in listed companies bought for cash (Equinor's 10% of Ørsted, $3.4bn): does the acquisition stop's listed-target limb (target below P3 → ADVERSE) apply, or only the worth-test limb for purchases of a listed holding's shares? This run read it as a holding-share purchase (CAUTION). The listed-target reading would turn SF to FAIL.
- 2026-10-08 · EQNR REVIEW — Commodity producers and P15 customer silence: an integrated oil major selling into liquid markets prints no >10%-customer statement, so silence flags `customer conc.`, and without dominance it becomes a STRONG customer-power force by rule. Should a market-priced, exchange-cleared commodity seller be able to evidence breadth from the market structure, or does the rule stand as written?
- 2026-10-08 · EQNR REVIEW — Geometric-mean growth read through loss years: with a base year ≤ 0 (FY2016 OI −224 after J3), read (i) is n.m. and fails P3, while read (ii) gives 18.3%. Confirm that a non-positive intermediate or base year makes read (i) n.m., not a telescoped endpoint rate.
- 2026-10-08 · INRN.SW REVIEW — P20 tier 1 with multi-year named projects: Interroll discloses total spend for new plants (Hiram USD 11m, Mosbach > EUR 40m, Suzhou CNY 181m) but no per-year split, so the constructed tier applies and the builds in flat-revenue years (FY2019–20) count wholly as maintenance; should a disclosed project total be spread over its disclosed construction years (straight-line, logged) as a tier-1 figure?
- 2026-10-08 · 300750.SZ REVIEW — Bank acceptance bills (CAS 应付票据) as supplier finance: Chinese filers pay suppliers with bank-accepted bills secured by margin deposits. Read as supplier-finance liabilities (out of NIBCL, into EV, margin deposits out of cash), CATL's worst-of-four ROCE is 8.3%; read as trade payables it is 10.0%, which decides Past. Should §2·1A/Gate 2 name a rule for bank-accepted trade bills (and commercial acceptances) so the call is repeatable across Chinese filers?
- 2026-10-08 · ZBRA REVIEW — Growth read through a negative year: the P3 geometric-mean read of ΔFCF/sh is n.m. when per-share FCF crosses zero mid-window (ZBRA FY2023, maintenance FCF −$117m on a $180m settlement payment and distributor destocking), which fails P3 even where the endpoint read is positive; should §2·1A state that a mid-window sign change makes read (i) n.m. (as applied here and in an earlier memo's OI read), or that read (i) telescopes to the endpoint ratio?
- 2026-10-08 · ZBRA REVIEW — Factored receivables: Zebra's "unremitted cash collections due to banks on factored accounts receivable" ($68–142m) sit in accrued liabilities and its cash; this run kept them in CE and added them to the debt-equivalents (the harsher reading). The Gate 2 debt-equivalent list does not name receivables factoring or the collections owed to the factor; a line would make the call repeatable.
- 2026-10-08 · GEBN.SW REVIEW — Sterilising purchases and ordinary repurchases in one worth reading: Geberit's off-programme purchases for employee plans (322,552 shares, ≈ 1% of shares, at CHF 513–566) read clearly above worth alongside a capital-reduction programme whose clearly-above slices are 0.69%. Read separately (V13 limb), the record is CAUTION; aggregated, ADVERSE. Should the materiality floor for the ordinary ADVERSE limb count sterilising tranches that are clearly above worth, or does V13's dilution test keep them apart?
- 2026-10-07 · CROX REVIEW — Acquisition-stop write-down limb, "large relative to the acquirer": no measure or bar is set. CROX's $737M is 40% of pre-charge equity and 72% of the prior year's OI but 8–10% of EV and 15% of total assets; the run (and a three-lens panel, unanimously) took the harsher reading under J13 → ADVERSE. A fixed measure (EV? equity? OI?) and bar would make the CAUTION/ADVERSE call repeatable — and should say whether a deal priced at or below worth at execution (here 10.3–11.1× pre-deal OI) can still reach ADVERSE on the write-down alone.
- 2026-10-07 · CROX REVIEW — Controller/insider-entity counterparty (§2·1C·2): the text says clearly above worth is ADVERSE but sets no time window, while ·3 treats clearly-above-worth purchases earlier in the P1 window as CAUTION. The run read the 2018 Blackstone preferred buyout under ·3's earlier-window rule (CAUTION); a literal reading makes it a time-unlimited hard strike. Clarify.
- 2026-10-07 · CROX REVIEW — P19 normalised reads at the owner's request: tranche reads on 5-year trailing OI (beside P19's mandated year and trailing-3 reads) and on the 3/5 years before the tranche (knowable at execution). Useful, but they cut both ways (boom-year buybacks look far worse, impairment-year buybacks better) and break across perimeter changes (an acquisition's debt in EV, pre-deal years in OI). Should P19 add a 5-year read and a perimeter rule, and should an impairment-depressed year read be context when the trailing reads agree?
- 2026-10-07 · CROX REVIEW — §7.1 reason precedence when two Past rules decide WEAK together (J6 decline whose outcome is WEAK only because leverage fails P6, and leverage itself): the run printed `leverage` (the sufficient cause); a literal reading prints `ROCE decline` first. Define "decide".
- 2026-10-07 · CROX REVIEW — P15 ready-vendor exception and contract manufacturing: the live text needs "named and qualified" vendors; footwear and apparel filers disclose production shares by rank but never names, so the exception is in practice closed to them, and the strong-moat route (evidenced share) rarely opens for brands. CROX moved Future ADEQUATE → WEAK on this reading (a three-lens panel, unanimous), against seven earlier versions. Should vendors identified by rank and share, with owned tooling and formulations, count as qualified, or is the strict reading intended?
- 2026-10-08 · CDR.WA REVIEW — P20 for game studios: capitalised development of a new title in a flat-revenue period is all maintenance under the constructed tier, so a build year drives maintenance-capex FCF to ~0 (CDR.WA FY2025: PLN 3.9m), which then fails P3 growth and turns ordinary board pay into an R9 proportionality strike (547% fiscal-year read). Should a disclosed new-title development spend (or development in progress on unreleased titles) count as named-project growth capex under tier 1, the way a new plant does?
- 2026-10-08 · NESN.SW REVIEW — Per-unit customer silence: a filer statement that no customer reaches 10% of *Group* revenue was read as silence at Zone level (a customer just under 10% of Group sales can be 26–51% of a Zone), flagging `customer conc.` in every Zone of a consumer-staples group; should §2·1B say whether a group-level statement carries to units, or set a threshold (e.g. the unit's share of group revenue) above which it does?
- 2026-10-08 · NESN.SW REVIEW — Segment-asset leg of P22 when the filer discloses no segment assets: this run read invested capital (PP&E plus working capital by segment, the filer's voluntary measure) and left out the separately disclosed goodwill and intangibles; adding them lifts Nestlé Health Science from 7.5% to about 18%. A line naming the measure would make the call repeatable.
- 2026-10-08 · NESN.SW REVIEW — Listed-subsidiary NCI at market where the venue is not served (Nigeria) or stale (Pakistan): this run used the filer's year-end market capitalisations; the Gate 2 text asks for market value at px_date. A fallback rule would help.
- 2026-10-08 · BKNG REVIEW — Customer concentration by silence on a marketplace (BKNG v4): accommodation partners pay about 30% of revenue as agency commissions across 4.4 million properties, and no filer statement rules out a 10% customer, so P15's silence rule flags `customer conc.`. Should a marketplace whose paying organisations number in the millions be able to clear the B2B silence test on a count of paying counterparties the filer prints, as the funding test reads insured deposits as many counterparties?
- 2026-10-08 · BKNG REVIEW — Pay-versus-performance as the hairiest number (BKNG v4): the CEO's compensation actually paid ($139.5m FY2023, $126.4m FY2024) is more than three times his Summary Compensation Table totals ($46.7m, $44.8m), because CAP marks unvested awards to the year-end share price. It alone decides the strike here; should the absolute-line strike read CAP when the grant-date total is below twice the line, or should CAP count only as a flag?
- 2026-10-08 · KNIN.SW REVIEW — Unit-level customer silence: Kuehne+Nagel states every year that no customer exceeds 10% of group revenue, but nothing at unit level. Read strictly (J13), that silence flags `customer conc.` in all four units and, with no dominant unit, becomes a STRONG customer-power force in each (Future S = 1.44, WEAK). Should a group-level "no customer > 10%" statement, from a filer with about 400,000 customers, count for units whose revenue is at least a third of the group's (Sea 36%, Air 30%), or should §2·1B name the unit-level test explicitly?
- 2026-10-08 · KNIN.SW REVIEW — Acquisition-stop proxy in a down year: IMC's only disclosed profit is its first full year after the deal (CHF 26m earnings, US drayage downcycle), and the B64(q) proxy reads it at about 47× deal EV, ADVERSE. Should the proxy read net earnings (as disclosed) or gross them up to an EBIT-equivalent, and should a post-deal year count as "pre-deal" when the filer withholds pre-deal figures?
- 2026-10-08 · ADBE REVIEW — Pay-versus-performance "compensation actually paid" as the absolute-limb year total: ADBE's FY2023 CAP ($127.74m, driven by the share price's rise on unvested awards; FY2025 CAP was −$17.39m) is the only figure above twice the ≈ $40m absolute line, so R9's "any other per-person total … compensation actually paid included" alone makes the pay limb a strike. Should P17 keep CAP as a strike-capable basis, or read it for flags only, since it marks unvested equity to market rather than measuring pay awarded or realised? (Here the verdict does not turn on it: the Semrush acquisition stop is an independent hard strike.)
- 2026-10-08 · BA.L REVIEW — Materiality of the clearly-above-worth slice: BAE's FY2023–H1 2026 repurchases are material (4.7% of diluted shares) and above worth, but only the H1 2026 tranche (0.4%) reads clearly above worth while the programme is live. This run applied the ≈ 1% floor to the clearly-above slice (CAUTION, not ADVERSE); applying it to the record as a whole would make the buybacks ADVERSE. The worth test could say which reading the floor takes.
- 2026-10-08 · ROG.SW REVIEW — A controlled listed subsidiary that sells mostly to its parent: Chugai takes 58% of its revenue from sister segments, so as a unit it would merge into Roche Pharmaceuticals (P22 sister-unit rule), yet as a controlled listed subsidiary it is material on its own revenue (11.1% of group revenue, intra-group sales included). Memo v2 kept it a separate part (the harsher reading, as it counts WEAK pending its memo). Should the controlled-subsidiary materiality line measure external revenue only, and does the sister-unit merge apply to a listed subsidiary?
- 2026-10-08 · IBKR REVIEW — Broker customer credit balances and the funding pairing: a self-clearing broker's customer credit balances ($177bn) finance its margin loans ($109bn) under the 15c3-3 reserve formula; this run read them as "other funding of a lending activity" (Gate 2 debt-equivalents, R2) on the harsher reading (J13), which makes the no-cash leg admit no positive price (trigger n.m.; the funding-out pairing gives $16.04). Should Gate 2 name brokerage customer balances explicitly — funding of the margin-lending activity, or customer funds of the brokerage product kept out of EV like segregated client funds are kept out of cash — and should the funding-concentration test ("a lender read on STD as a whole") reach a broker whose largest revenue line is margin interest?
- 2026-10-08 · IBKR REVIEW — Customer statements that cover one revenue line: IBKR states no customer exceeds 2% of commissions, but commissions are 35% of net revenue and interest revenue is silent; this run treated the silence as concentration (R7) and carried it as a contained STRONG force. Should §2·1B say whether a statement covering one revenue line rules a >10% customer out where the other lines are customer-specific (margin interest), or set a coverage share?
- 2026-10-08 · IBKR REVIEW — P17 denominator for an Up-C: the parent holds 26% of IBG LLC; consolidated NI and FCF put CEO pay at ≤ 2.3% of the line, parent-attributable figures at 8.4% (FY) and 10.7% (trailing). The group 1C denominator (attributable) was applied by analogy as the harsher reading although IBKR is not a group under §2; MCap meanwhile counts every economic unit. A line naming the Up-C denominator would make the call repeatable.
- 2026-10-08 · NMAN.ST REVIEW — J6 limb (a) on a near-flat step: beginning-of-period ROCE_wc went 13.7% → 13.6% → 12.6% → 12.2% → 9.0% (FY2021–25), so a 0.1-point dip counts as the first of four "declines"; the ROCE_xc (beg) series fires on three clear declines anyway here, but should limb (a) carry a minimum step (e.g. rounding to the printed decimal, or a relative threshold) so that noise cannot complete a run?
- 2026-10-08 · NMAN.ST REVIEW — Acquisition-stop materiality line "more than 1% of the acquirer's EV": this run read it on today's EV (≈ SEK 72m), the harsher reading, which pulled the FY2024 Duroair + Olicem aggregate (SEK 79.2m) into the test; on the deal-time EV (≈ SEK 9bn in 2024) it would fall below. Should the rule name which EV (deal-time, as knowable at execution, or the memo's)?
- 2026-10-12 · NVS REVIEW — Spin-offs inside the P1 window (NVS: Alcon 2019, Sandoz 2023): MODEL is silent on whether the consolidated record reads each year on its own filed perimeter or on the filer's restated continuing-operations flows against unrestated balance sheets; NVS v2 used the filed perimeter and logged the restated reading (lower) as a sensitivity. A rule would fix the reading for every spin-off name.
- 2026-10-13 · OXIG.L ANALYSE — Unit-level customer breadth when only a group statement exists: Oxford Instruments states no customer exceeds 10% of group revenue, which bounds any customer at about 13% of the 74% Imaging & Analysis unit but says nothing for the 26% Advanced Technologies unit. This run read both units as silent (flagged) and used the group bound as containment evidence for I&A only. A line on whether the bound a group statement implies for a unit counts as a filer statement for that unit (it clears the unit only when the unit is 100% of revenue) would keep such calls repeatable.
- 2026-10-18 · CNR.TO ANALYSE — TTM maintenance capex when the annual tier is disclosed but the interims print no split: CN's FY2025 maintenance FCF (tier 1, capex less disclosed strategic capex) is C$4,791m, but the TTM figure falls to the constructed tier (C$3,831m) because the interims carry no split, and TTM then governs as the lower reading. The tier switch alone moves the FCF leg of the trigger by about 20%. A line on whether a TTM may carry the latest annual disclosed growth share forward (pro rata), or must always construct, would keep TTM readings repeatable across filers.
- 2026-10-14 · DOV REVIEW — Decentralised multi-industrials: Dover groups its operating companies into five segments by business model and go-to-market, and discloses revenue only by segment and geography. Under §2 Gate 0 a segment whose named businesses differ in paying customer is irreducible when shares are not printed (J13), so CST (supermarket refrigeration, HVAC heat exchangers, can-making machinery) and EP (vehicle service, defence electronics) fail. The same reading likely fails most diversified industrials (DCI, AME, ITW-type portfolios) on disclosure form rather than on understandability. Should the test credit a filer-stated common business model for a segment, or does it stand as written?
- 2026-10-14 · AME REVIEW — Diversified instrument segments: AMETEK reports each of its two segments as a "process/automation" line and an "aerospace" line, and §2 Gate 0 fails both as irreducible on paying customer, although v4.5.1 reads industry at the filer's segment description ("analytical, test and measurement instruments"). Should a segment whose lines share one industry and one moat class (spec-in switching costs) but sell to different organisational customers pass the one-level test, or does the paying-customer limb stand as written?
- 2026-10-19 · BMI REVIEW — Live-year straddle in the worth test: the H1 2026 tranche reads 24.5×/23.9× EV/OI and 27.3× MCap/FCF on the TTM year read (just under the clearly-above-worth lines) but 26.2×/25.6× and 30.0× on the trailing-3 reads; the run let the trailing reads govern (in doubt, the less flattering), which makes the slice clearly above worth and the finding ADVERSE rather than CAUTION. Should §2·1C·3 name which read governs a live tranche when its TTM year read and trailing reads sit either side of the clearly-above line?
- 2026-10-19 · TROW REVIEW — G6 and a consolidated fund's securities book inside CFO: when a consolidated investment product's securities are a G6 class, does the exclude reading also take the book's net purchases out of CFO (the capital moving with the income), or does CFO stay as the filer prints it? TROW v8 keeps GAAP CFO on both readings (Appendix C named line); the alternative cannot lift Past there, but it could on a name whose growth turns on it.
- 2026-10-19 · CCL-B.TO ANALYSE — Customer-breadth arithmetic: a consolidated top-2/top-5 customer table can rule out a >10% customer for the largest unit by bounding (CCL-B.TO, 2026-10-19), but never for smaller units; a line in §2·1B would make the bounding step repeatable.
- 2026-10-19 · COH.AX ANALYSE — Geographic reportable segments as P22 units: a single-product company reporting by region (COH.AX, 2026-10-19) becomes a three-unit group with three near-identical Future blocks; a line in §2 on whether a geographic segment of one business is a unit would make the grain repeatable.
- 2026-10-20 · LULU REVIEW — Regional segments of a single-brand retailer (Americas, China Mainland, Rest of World) meet P22 and so make the company a group under G3/G4: unit sentences, a weighted Future and the allocation-across-the-parts test (INDETERMINATE (b) where segment assets are not disclosed). Should a segmentation that is purely geographic, the same products sold to the same kind of paying customer, count as units for the group rules?
- 2026-10-20 · LULU REVIEW — The R9 hairiest number read on pay-versus-performance CAP: McDonald's FY2023 CAP of $46.9m (SCT $16.5m) breaches the ≈ $40m absolute line mainly through share-price gains on earlier grants. Is a CAP-driven breach of the absolute limb, with every SCT total far inside it, the intended flag?
- 2026-10-20 · LULU REVIEW — Earlier-window deals: a target with no positive pre-deal OI (Mirror, 2020) was read as clearly above worth under J13, making the earlier-window CAUTION fire because the then interim co-CFO is still CFO. Should "no positive pre-deal OI" count as clearly above worth for the earlier-window limb, as it does as "at least CAUTION" in the trailing window?
- 2026-10-20 · BXB.AX ANALYSE — Recoveries for customers' lost pooled equipment: Brambles books compensation for irrecoverable pallets (US$191.5–227.5m a year, FY2023–FY2026) as proceeds from sale of PP&E in investing, while the net gain sits in OI; under gross capex (P20) the cash stays outside FCF, and BXB.AX's SF verdict turned on it (FY2025 trailing MCap/FCF 29.5 gross, 21.8 netted). A line in §2·1A on whether such recoveries net against maintenance capex would make the reading repeatable.
- 2026-10-20 · BXB.AX ANALYSE — Group customer statements and unit breadth: a filer statement that no customer exceeds 10% of group revenue does not rule one out within a reportable segment, so every geographic unit is flagged (BXB.AX, 2026-10-20; COH.AX, 2026-10-19, had no statement at all). A line in §2·1B on whether a group-level statement carries to units that sell the same service would make the grain repeatable.
- 2026-10-20 · TNE.AX ANALYSE — V13 materiality window: employee-trust purchases of 0.23% (FY2025) and 0.85% (FY2026 live year) were summed over the trailing three FYs plus the live year (1.08%, material) and, with the diluted count rising, read ADVERSE. Read year by year, both tranches sit below the ≈1% floor and nothing is flagged. Should §2·1C·3 state that the floor is tested on the summed recent record (the reading the register's memos apply), or per tranche-year?
- 2026-10-20 · TNE.AX ANALYSE — J6 limb (a) on ROCE_wc for a cash-accumulating company: the end-of-period ROCE_wc fell five years running (41.5% → 31.6%) only because cash and term deposits tripled, while ROCE_xc held at 78–83% and every level sits at twice the P2 bar. The decline caps Past at ADEQUATE. Should the cash-accumulation cause be able to clear limb (a) when the ex-cash series shows no decline?
- 2026-10-21 · PYPL REVIEW — Customer funds of a payment or e-money company that is not deposit-taking (PayPal's $40bn of funds payable to customers): the run kept them in capital employed on the gift-card-float wording of §2·1A and the float interest booked in revenue (ROCE 6.0%; netted as NIBCL it would be 12.6%). A ruling would fix the reading across payment names (WISE.L, ADYEN-type filers) and say whether the safeguarded assets and their interest are the operating book.
- 2026-10-21 · PYPL REVIEW — R2 lending-book rule for originate-to-sell loans booked in CFO (held for sale): the run measured the ±3% allowance on the prior year-end total loan book (held for investment plus held for sale); the rule does not say which book is the base when only the held-for-sale flows run through CFO.
- 2026-10-21 · 1913.HK REVIEW — HKEX filers print no per-person pay table for non-director executives, only the "five highest paid individuals" aggregate and HKD bands plus a key-management aggregate. The run read the top non-director earner as an executive officer (key management personnel, the harsher reading) and bounded the person's total from the pair aggregate less the second person's band. A ruling would fix whether key management personnel count as executive officers under R9 and whether band-derived bounds are the floor and ceiling of §2·1C·1.
- 2026-10-21 · SAAB-B.ST ANALYSE — §2·1C·3 earlier-window floor: LTI-hedging repurchases clearly above worth in FY2018–FY2020 were 0.43%, 0.75% and 0.85% of diluted shares year by year (each below the ≈1% floor) and 2.05% summed; summed, they are a CAUTION while the chair who oversaw them is in office. Should the earlier-window limb test the floor on a summed span (as applied here and in TNE.AX), and over which span?
- 2026-10-21 · AZN.L ANALYSE — P20 tier 3 in a year of clinical-stage deals: FY2024's business combinations (Gracell, Fusion) exceeded 1% of EV and the filer printed no acquired revenue, so the constructed tier was unavailable and the year took all capex, although the targets had no revenue (no growth capex could be created by them). Should a deal year whose targets are disclosed as pre-revenue (clinical stage) keep the constructed tier, or does the rule stand as written?
- 2026-10-21 · AZN.L ANALYSE — Listed-subsidiary NCI at market for an immaterial subsidiary on a venue whose share count is unreachable for routine clients (AstraZeneca Pharma India, 75%; BSE, NSE and the company site refused): the run carried the NCI at book and logged that the binding FCF leg cannot move. Should Gate 2 name a fallback (book, or the subsidiary's filed market capitalisation) when the subsidiary is below P22 materiality?
- 2026-10-22 · BBWI REVIEW — A spin-off parent's own 10-K recasts its income statements and the prior year-end balance sheet to continuing operations but leaves the cash-flow statement unsegregated (BBWI FY2021: "cash flows related to discontinued operations have not been segregated"). The run read the spin year's FCF as n/a, a history limit like the short window, and ran the FCF tests on the following four years. A ruling would fix whether that year is history-limited for FCF, or whether a derived continuing FCF (total CFO less the disclosed discontinued items) is admissible and labelled.
- 2026-10-22 · BBWI REVIEW — §2·1A lists deferred income/revenue in the default NIBCL, and also names gift-card float as an ambiguous item that stays in CE when material. For a retailer whose deferred revenue is mainly gift cards and loyalty points ($223m, 5% of CE), the run kept it in CE, the harsher reading. A one-line ruling would settle which list governs.
- 2026-10-22 · TRI.PA REVIEW — The ΔFCF/sh geometric read turns n.m. (and fails P3) for the whole P1 window on one negative maintenance-capex FCF year caused by a working-capital swing (Trigano FY2024: −€6.5m, after a €382.6m inventory build), while the endpoint read is +13.5%. The same year also triggers the P17 loss-year pay flag. Question for Jakub: should a single negative FCF year inside a ten-year window make the geometric read n.m., or should the harsher-of rule then fall back to the endpoint read?
- 2026-10-22 · SEB-A.ST ANALYSE — First FI-lens memo: the acquisition stop's "1% of the acquirer's EV" has no EV under FI (EV/OI n.m.); the run used market cap (the harsher, smaller denominator, J13) for AirPlus (SEK 5.2bn, 1.7%). Should §2·1C·3 name the FI materiality base (market cap, or equity)?
- 2026-10-22 · SEB-A.ST ANALYSE — FI funding test on a universal bank: deposits from non-financial and financial corporations are capital-market funding by the R7 text, so every large corporate bank's capital-market channel exceeds half of liabilities (SEB 58.5% on the most favourable reading). Is that the intended reach of `funding conc.` under FI, or should the corporate operating-deposit share be read separately?
- 2026-10-22 · SEB-A.ST ANALYSE — Customer silence (P15) applied to a bank's large-corporate unit: no bank states that no borrower pays over 10% of revenue, so every corporate banking unit is flagged `customer conc.` by silence. Should a bank's large-exposure disclosure (limits against own funds) count as the filer statement?
- 2026-10-22 · REH.AX ANALYSE — A multi-year grant counted in full in its grant year (R9) stays in the three-year pay table for three tests, so one award fixes an SF FAIL until it rolls out, whatever later pay looks like. Should the memo state the roll-out year as the flip date (here the FY2028 table), or should a disclosed "equivalent to five years of annual grant value" award also be read per year for the trailing test?
- 2026-10-22 · REH.AX ANALYSE — P20 deflators: ABS moved to a monthly CPI, whose June-year annual change (FY2026 3.8%) differs from the old June-quarter series (FY2025 2.1% quarterly vs 1.9% monthly). Should P20 name which ABS series governs, and should the project keep a sourced CPI table per currency so routine runs stop re-deriving it?

## ▶ Group dependencies

*INTAKE maintains this block (MODEL §2 Group dependencies; §8.7 item 6; `routines/INTAKE.md` step 3). **v4.6.0 (G4): a dependency never stops its parent.** A group run (a parent with a material controlled listed subsidiary or listed holding) completes with a part whose memo is missing or stale read as ungradeable (WEAK with its weight in the Future, nothing added to the look-through Past, no credit) and lists it; INTAKE adds `` `HKEY` — dependency of PARENT (run {D}) ``, removes it when HKEY's ANALYSE or REVIEW memo lands, and then adds `` `PARENT` — parent re-run owed (HKEY memo {date}) `` for REVIEW priority 1c. ANALYSE takes an unmarked key with no memo first; REVIEW takes a stale one (memo older than 15 months, written below MODEL v4.5.0, or a DNAF memo written below v4.6.0) at priority 1c. A marked key stays ungradeable for its parents. `INCOMPLETE (dependency: KEY)` and verdict invariance are retired; before v4.6.0 a line was added when a group run ended `INCOMPLETE (dependency: KEY)`.*

## ▶ Method re-tests v4.6.0

*Gate-0, lens and group rules that reopen DNAF names and void DNAF expectations (MODEL v4.6.0: Jakub's decisions G1–G6 of 2026-10-15; `queue/IDEAS.md` §G0), effective at "commit model". REVIEW reads this block at priority 1b before `▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0` — held names first, from both blocks, then the top line first; INTAKE removes a key's line when that key's ANALYSE or REVIEW memo written under MODEL v4.6.0 or later lands, and marks it when the run ends INCOMPLETE (MODEL §8.7 item 6). A memo below v4.6.0 re-tests Gate 0, the lens, the group rules and the G6 pairing on v4.6.0, and a prior DNAF on a retired ground runs the full analysis (MODEL §8.2 step 3); a key that also has a `▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0` line takes that re-test in the same run. The v4.6.0 reading: the understanding statement is the best sentence the filings allow, and Gate 0 fails only for incomprehension or obfuscation (G1, G2); the irreducible-unit test with its J13 named-without-shares limb, the material-"Other" fail, the holdings-cycle fail and the R4 too-hard pile retire, the three too-hard cases reading on STD with its conservative rules (G2); the material unit (P22) is the grain, each with its own sentence and Future block, and a unit that mixes businesses, or an "Other", is described naming its parts and graded as one unit (G3); conglomerates, trading houses and holding companies are analysed at the parent — consolidated Past, each material part's Future, SF with the allocation across the parts (`reinvestment`) — and a part never fails the group nor stops the parent (G4). In Gate 1A, every G6 class above the P31 line (equity-accounted associates and JVs with their long-term interests, H excepted; S3 stakes, securities and non-operating loans; an insurance activity's whole investment portfolio) is read exclude-both and include-both, the harsher governing (G6); the G6 sub-heading lists the names the landing scan found above the line. Each line reads `KEY` — the memo and its retired v4.5.x ground → run the full analysis (G1–G4) → where the worked figures sit. A projection, where one exists, is the 2026-10-14 Gate-0 review's reading if Gate 0 opened, on the v4.5.3 rules below it: a pointer, never a finding; Appendix G of each memo records the move. A verdict change goes to Jakub for the merge.*

**Jakub's review request (2026-10-20) — first after held names.** Eight AVOID-1 names Jakub asked to have reviewed, read in this order; each prior is below v4.6.0, so its REVIEW re-tests every v4.5.0 rule its prior predates and Gate 0, the lens, the group rules and the G6 pairing of v4.6.0 (MODEL §8.2 step 3). Notes are pointers, never findings.

## ▶ Method re-tests v4.5.0

*Codified rules that can move a verdict (MODEL v4.5.0: Jakub's decisions J1–J15 of 2026-10-06 as revised by his round-2 decisions R1–R14 and V1–V18 of 2026-10-07; `queue/IDEAS.md` §T), effective at "commit model". REVIEW reads this block as priority 1b, top line first; INTAKE removes a key's line when that key's REVIEW memo of v4.5.0 or later lands, and marks it when the run ends INCOMPLETE (MODEL §8.7 item 6). Order: held names; upward moves (expected, then possible); WATCH → AVOID by confidence (≥ 80%, then 60–75%); outcome open (below 60%); verdict-word changes to DNAF last. **Re-scanned line by line under round 2 on 2026-10-07**, from memo evidence (Appendices B, D and E) and the round-2 area specs: each line reads `KEY` — the rules that bite: what the memo shows → expected (confidence). The figures are memo text ("verify" where the memo lacks one; "ext." marks outside knowledge, never used as a number): pointers for the REVIEW, never findings. The round-2 reading: two lenses, and the too-hard pile (Gate 0 FAIL, DNAF) for a bank or insurer that does not fit FI, a group with a material non-captive insurer or deposit-funded bank, and anything neither lens reads (R4); captive finance arms consolidated on STD (R2); groups on the consolidated Past and the OI-weighted Future (P29; R3, R5); material listed holdings at their own intrinsic value (V14); maintenance-capex FCF in every FCF test, the all-capex reading printed beside it (P20, R6); the P28 one-off down year for price, the trigger, the ADD band and the sell line, with mitigating facts in the sell evaluation (R1, V11); customer and supplier concentration flagged and judged against the moat, a concentration without a strong moat being a STRONG force under P16 (R7); pay on the hairiest number, P17's lines and the director cap as flags and a strike only when egregious, more than twice the line (R9, V3); pledges and sales never flags, the alignment call (P23) and votes versus economics inputs that act only at strike-logic step 3, where a single flag fails (`alignment`) with NOT ALIGNED management and separated votes (R8, V4); sterilisation below about 1% of shares logged, ADVERSE only when egregious and dilutive (V13); acquisitions against worth and the glaring-RPT strike confirmed (V1, V17); the trigger at the no-cash bound (R10). Worth yardsticks (P19, STD): at or below about 14× EV/OI or 16× MCap/FCF is at worth; about 25× EV/OI or 28.6× MCap/FCF is clearly above it; where a tranche's reads straddle a yardstick the less flattering governs when in doubt, the FCF read being the maintenance-capex one. Flags count per distinct behaviour (repurchases, deals, executive pay, director fees). A verdict change goes to Jakub for the merge. Label-only, section-only and band-only moves are not listed here (`▶ Routed by the MODEL v4.5.0 build` below). **Re-read under MODEL v4.6.0 (2026-10-15):** the too-hard pile and the one-level decomposition are retired (G2, G3) and a missing dependency never stops a parent (G4), so no line below expects DNAF or `INCOMPLETE (dependency)` any more; the lines that did (BRK.B, ULVR.L, CSU.TO and the R4 and decomposition names) are rewritten in place, and the DNAF ones also have a line in `▶ Method re-tests v4.6.0`, which REVIEW reads first.*

## ▶ Routed from the AMZN v3 fresh ANALYSE, register v294 (2026-09-13)

1. **`AMZN` — FY2025 realized compensation not retrieved (P17 logged INDETERMINATE).** The 2026 proxy discloses a realized-compensation figure beside the Summary Compensation Table total; only the SCT total was sourced (**Jassy $2.1m**; no equity granted to any NEO in 2025, none to the CEO since 2021). Realized comp rises with the share price and is the **only** route by which the ≈US$40m indexed absolute-scrutiny trigger could fire. Proportionality is not at issue — it passes by roughly three orders of magnitude on both reads. **Named re-test: the 2027 DEF 14A (~Apr 2027).** Under §2·1C an INDETERMINATE finding can never complete a flag pair, so this cannot fail SF on its own.
2. **`AMZN` — FY2025 director compensation totals not retrieved.** Amazon pays non-employee directors in restricted stock with no cash retainer; Bezos and Jassy take none. Historical grant levels sit well inside the US 2026 stops (chair/lead ≈$1.02m, ordinary ≈$672k), so a hard strike is implausible, but the figure is owed and the memo's Appendix E flag stays open until it is sourced. **Re-test: 2027 DEF 14A.**
3. **`AMZN` — P19 CAUTION re-test; SF now one flag from failing.** SF PASSes on a single flag (capital allocation CAUTION); one further flag pairs and fails the gate. Discriminating evidence for the next touch, in order: (a) whether any repurchase authorisation is **created or executed** — there has been none since the single $6.0bn FY2022 tranche, and conjunct (iii) of ADVERSE turns on exactly this; (b) whether FY2026 capex lands near the ~$200bn guide; (c) whether the debt-funded share keeps rising — long-term debt face went **$68,836m → $132,995m in H1 2026** on $66,998m of gross issuance across USD/EUR/CHF/CAD; (d) whether AWS growth and the ~36.8% trailing segment margin hold through the ramp. **Triggers: Q3 2026 results (30 Oct 2026), FY2026 10-K (~Feb 2027), 2027 proxy.**
4. **`AMZN` — current lease liabilities and short-term debt are not disclosed on the balance-sheet face.** Amazon presents only three current-liability lines (payables, accrued expenses and other, unearned revenue). The current portion of long-term debt ($2,748m at FY2025 year-end, from the Q1 2026 debt note), short-term debt and current lease liabilities all sit inside "accrued expenses and other". Two consequences, both disclosed rather than corrected because correcting requires estimating an undisclosed input: **(i)** NIBCL is overstated and capital employed understated by an estimated $16–19bn, biasing ROCE **up** by ~0.4pp on a $600bn base — against conservatism, and immaterial to the gate (worst-of-four 11.1% vs a 10% floor); **(ii)** the headline EV uses only the primary-sourced debt stack of $226,562m and omits ~$25,073m, which **flatters** the band by roughly $2–3 — the conservative stack is printed alongside in Appendix F. **Closure: the FY2026 10-K lease note (current operating and finance lease liabilities) and debt note.**
5. **`AMZN` — `stockanalysis.com` carries a restated FY2022 operating income.** It prints **13,348** against the filed **12,248**. The series was therefore **rejected** for the P1 window under §4's exact-tie-out rule and the decade was rebuilt from `marketbeat.com`, which tied out *exactly* against the FY2025 Q4 8-K on both covered years (revenue, OI, equity, and the current- and non-current-liability composites). Recorded so a later session does not silently re-adopt the restated series. The underlying SEC-retrieval constraint is already logged under the PYPL v5 block below (items 3–4) and is **not duplicated here** — but note it bit again: every primary this session required its own locator search, and the 8-K earnings-release exhibits were again the cheap route to full statements.
6. **`AMZN` — other acquired intangibles not retrieved.** Tangible book value is computed as equity less goodwill only, which slightly **overstates** TBV. Immaterial against $411bn of equity and $23.3bn of goodwill; both qualifiers fail by a wide margin either way. Backfill from the goodwill-and-intangibles note at the next touch.

### D. Method notes from the runs — retrieval routes that worked or failed (append to the retrieval doctrine)

- **FX:** `https://api.frankfurter.dev/v1/{YYYY-MM-DD}?from={BASE}&to={CCY}` — ECB euro reference rates; use the `.dev` host and `/v1/` path, percent-encode commas, **record the returned `date` field** (TARGET holidays return the prior publication). Label cells "ECB ref, {date}", never as a venue close. Fallback `open.er-api.com/v6/latest/{base}` is latest-only. `www.ecb.europa.eu` itself is refused from the cloud sandbox.
- **Google Finance price route:** the `Closed:` prefix is US-only; for every other venue the discriminator is **stamp time ≥ the venue's session end** (Taipei 05:30 UTC, Tokyo 06:00, Hong Kong ~08:10). **Always fetch with a unique cache-buster query parameter** — 9 of ~70 bare fetches returned months-old quotes with plausible magnitudes (ADBE 246.00 vs 257.26; NU 12.99 vs 15.33; TRI 86.04 vs 98.81). Tencent key is `0700:HKG` (`700:HKG` 404s). Provider snapshot stamps (MarketScreener `HH:MM:SS YYYY-MM-DD`) date the page, not the trade. A guessed numeric provider ID returned Per Aarsleff for a Carlsberg request — **record the instrument name with every price**.
- **Provider market caps use the ISSUED share count (incl. treasury); value per share on the NET count** — tested exactly on Royal Unibrew (issued 49,300,000; treasury 1,236,387 = 2.5%; provider cap reconciles to 0.02% on issued). One division per row — `price × shares = displayed market cap` — caught two real stale counts (`DNP.WA`, `BKNG`) that no identity check can catch (ISIN-stable splits are invisible to the §7.1a prior-check). Make it standing on every SWEEP (IDEAS C5). MarketScreener's Diageo market cap was 38% off on this test.
- **SEC:** the R-file route `…/{cik}/{accession-no-dashes}/R{n}.htm` renders one statement per file — fetch `FilingSummary.xml` first (lists every R-file with ShortName); **`R1.htm` is the cover page and the cheapest primary share count**. No R-file exists for Item 5; the 10-K XBRL instance truncates inside its context definitions. EDGAR full-text search is **ranked and truncated — a nil return is not evidence of absence**; `/cgi-bin/browse-edgar` is robots-disallowed; company IR filing stores are the "has this been filed" route (two independent store reads = strongest available evidence, still not proof). In the cloud sandbox `www.sec.gov/Archives/` is reachable by the fetch tool but not by `curl`.
- **Robots/host limits found:** `brand.adyen.com` (Adyen statements PDFs), CAREL's IR file server (unparseable binary), `release.tdnet.info`, `investors.hexagon.com`, `s204.q4cdn.com`, `assets.asics.com`, `api.alertir.com`, `mb.cision.com`. Clean: Intuit, PSI, InPost, Symbotic IR stores; kabupro for EDINET (standing).
- **Standing rules worth keeping:** one issuer per corporate-action query (grouped queries return nothing); carry the **new** date, never the old one with "(extended)"; before recording a single-figure finding that contradicts the corpus, re-fetch the row as printed with headers and units (a 10× unit misread on NKE's cash-flow line was caught this way); never infer a historical balance-sheet movement from a forward-looking guidance line (CAREL/Cotes); a supersession that drops a section without saying so is not a supersession.

### E. Scheduled-run architecture — findings that supersede the "Overnight / delayed-run design" sketch below (detail: `queue/runs/2026-09-09_scheduled-runs-postmortem-and-workflow-fix_v1.md`)

- **The block is per-session repo attachment, not GitHub.** In the cloud sandbox `api.github.com` returns 403 "GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session. Use `add_repo`"; `add_repo` does not exist in the tool registry; `git push --dry-run` returns 403 "not in this session's authorized repository set" — **the PAT is stripped and irrelevant to the write path.** `git clone` / `raw.githubusercontent.com` reads work with the PAT; a command classifier blocks some `bash` shapes non-deterministically. None of the six scheduled tasks had a repository source; all six were disabled 2026-09-09 13:37 UTC.
- **Fix (user actions):** recreate the tasks with the repo attached as a GitHub source (or device-bound); collapse six runs to one daily (~07:30–09:00 UTC, after Asia settles) + one weekly integrity run; prompts point at `queue/TODO.md`, name stop conditions, and never write working state to project knowledge; **hard stop: no live `MODEL.md` read → no verdict, no memo, no register version** (bootstrap v1.1.0 change — manual paste, framework gate); mirror MODEL v4.2.0 into project knowledge for mobile reading; add `queue/TODO.md` and `queue/IDEAS.md` to BOOTSTRAP §CONFIG; **rotate/revoke the PAT** (now in ~27 transcripts; open since 2026-08-27).

## Coverage audit — global top 100 by market cap (snapshot 2026-08-28)

*Source: companiesmarketcap.com global ranking, retrieved 2026-08-28 (sourced data; ranks only — **no market-cap figures are carried into this file**, and the ranking itself is a third-party aggregation, tier-3 under §4, adequate for a coverage checklist and for nothing else). Matched against all 317 register rows on **company name, not the ticker key** (§7.1a) — cross-listings resolved, so Roche→`ROG.SW`, Novartis→`NVS`, Tencent→`0700.HK`, TSMC→`2330.TW`, Novo Nordisk→`NOVO-B.CO` all count as covered.*

**Result: 42 of the top 100 carry a register row; 58 do not.** The gap is not random — it is concentrated in exactly the three groups the project has never seeded: mega-cap banks and insurers, the semiconductor complex, and state-controlled Asian and Gulf issuers.

**Framing, stated plainly: this is a coverage audit, not a candidate screen.** Size is neither a quality nor a cheapness signal, and nothing here has been pre-judged on any gate. The value of working the list is that each absence becomes a recorded decision instead of an oversight — an AVOID row saves future time under §7.1 exactly as a WATCH row does.

### Tier B — financials, FI lens (MODEL v4.5.0 J4) — 12 names

**Each runs the Gate 0 lens test first (MODEL v4.5.0, J4).** A licensed bank or insurer, or the parent of a group supervised as one, that publishes a capital ratio covering ≥ 90% of consolidated assets takes the FI lens: ROE and capital tests (P24), MCap/NI ≤ 8, the P25 band. A bank or insurer with a material non-financial part, or without that coverage, is too hard (R4: Gate 0 FAIL, DNAF). The §2·1A substitution rule of the KSPI v1→v2 lesson (TODO v13) is retired. Every name here is a deposit- or premium-funded balance sheet. **Superseded in part by MODEL v4.6.0 (G2):** the too-hard pile is retired; a bank or insurer that does not fit FI is read on STD with its conservative rules, never DNAF.

## ANALYSE queue re-seed — candidate universe (built 2026-08-28)

*Answers the standing re-seed question below, option (c). **181 names, none of which has a register row** — de-duplicated against all 317 rows live at the 2026-08-28 snapshot, on company name per §7.1a, not on the ticker key. **Re-verified 2026-08-31 against the two rows added since** — SEE.L (Seeing Machines) and FLUT (Flutter Entertainment): neither appears in either block, and a core-name sweep of all 319 live company names against both blocks returns no collision. Tickers are suggested keys, resolved and verified with ISIN at run time. Selection rule applied: understandable business · long continuous record under a stable perimeter · structurally decent returns or a durable niche · family/founder/anchor control welcome. Screened out at source: utilities and regulated networks (standing screen), pure-play miners, oil & gas E&P, REITs, closed-end funds and pure investment holdings, SPAC-derived names, and anything with under ~5 years of standalone statements. **No figures are carried here by design** — nothing below is sourced financial data, and no name below has been valued, screened for cheapness, or pre-judged on any gate.*

## Overnight / delayed-run design (draft — for later recycling)

Design sketch for an unattended runner that processes this queue on a schedule. Not built yet; finalise later.

**Substrate.** Claude Code in headless mode (`claude -p`) driven by a **scheduled GitHub Action** (`on: schedule: cron`). This reuses the existing repo-native pipeline — `MODEL.md` as the operative framework, `_REGISTER.md` as state, §7.4a as the commit protocol — rather than re-plumbing it into a bespoke script. Same agent loop (bash, web fetch, git), no human in the loop. Alternatives: plain `cron` on a local/cloud box running the same `claude -p` call, or Claude Code Routines for clock-triggered runs. Cost for a nightly list of a few dozen names is single-digit dollars.

**Flow.**
1. Runner reads this file's queue section (mode + name).
2. Session-start: load PAT from secret, fetch live `MODEL.md` + `_REGISTER.md`.
3. Process each name **strictly sequentially** through the five-gate pipeline.
4. Commit each memo, then merge the register — one runner, sequential writes only.
5. Clear processed names from the queue; emit a JSON run summary (`--output-format json`, per-name verdict + `total_cost_usd`).

**Non-negotiable guardrails for unattended operation.**
- **Data-completeness gate.** If the inputs for the four CE variants / cheapness legs are not cleanly sourced, emit an `INCOMPLETE` stub memo flagged for human review and skip the verdict. Never fill a gap with an assumption to finish a row.
- **Sequential register I/O.** No fan-out. Parallel jobs PUT-ing `_REGISTER.md` manufacture exactly the collisions §7.4a exists to catch.
- **MODEL-mode lockout.** Runner handles ANALYSE / REVIEW / SWEEP only. Framework changes stay a human-only, exact-phrase "commit model" gate; runner has no write access to `model/` (was `instructions/` before MODEL v4.2.1).
- **Commit-time compliance re-measure** (v4.2.0): P13 word count, §7.1 cell caps, four-gates-only check, no register-wide superlatives — unattended runs drift ~10–65% over P13 and their own in-run compliance claims are not evidence.
- Secrets (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, PAT) as repo secrets, never in workflow files; `--allowedTools` scoped to EDGAR / Yahoo / GitHub.
- **Retrieval doctrine from the Aug-2026 runs:** primary results releases are the backbone (a Q4 release carries two full years of statements in one document and survives the URL-must-be-surfaced-by-search constraint); tier-2 columns must tie to a primary figure before their headers are trusted; per-filer productive channels are logged in each memo's App. A (e.g. Hitachi results library; Panasonic press-release PDFs; MHI tanshin archive; Lagardère interim reports; Trigano results releases). **US-domestic-filer addendum (FLUT, 2026-08-31):** for SEC filers the **10-Q is the efficient primary route**, not the 10-K — Item 1 Financial Statements is the first content in a 10-Q and therefore inside the fetch window, while a 10-K's Item 8 sits ~80 pages past it and truncates away. A 10-Q's comparative columns carry the prior fiscal year-end balance sheet in full, which alone satisfies three of the four §4 tie-out anchors plus the net-financial-position composite. Also logged: in this sandbox `bash` cannot reach `data.sec.gov` (proxy `host_not_allowed`) and `web_fetch` rejects any URL not first surfaced by a search, so the XBRL companyfacts/companyconcept endpoints are unavailable — plan the retrieval around searchable filing URLs.

**The real bottleneck is data, not scheduling.** The SEC-filer path is deterministic and safe to automate. The European and Japanese non-SEC names depend on primary-report PDF extraction — the fragile, judgment-heavy path where an unattended run is most likely to silently produce a wrong verdict. Harden those fetchers (or route them to `INCOMPLETE`) before trusting overnight output on this universe.

## Flags


- **Korean primary filings are unreachable from the sandbox (logged 2026-09-04, 244920.KS v1):** `dart.fss.or.kr` and `wcomp/comp.fnguide.com` refuse on robots, `kind.krx.co.kr` on bot detection, the issuer's own IR site on robots; `englishdart` was not reachable either. Every `.KS` ANALYSE in the re-seed block (9 names) and any KSPI-style Korean-listed name will hit the same wall — tier-2 series can be built (stockanalysis/S&P carries 5y for KRX) but cannot be accepted under §4. **Options for the user:** (a) an OpenDART API key (`opendart.fss.or.kr`, free registration) — would need `opendart.fss.or.kr` added to the sandbox allow-list; (b) Korean primaries uploaded to the chat as PDF/HTML for the session; (c) accept provisional tier-2 memos for Korean names with a standing rebuild flag, as done for 244920.KS. Until decided, Korean ANALYSE output carries a provisional label.
- **⚠ USER DECISION — rotate the GitHub PAT.** Three independent retrieval agents, working on unrelated names and none prompted to look, each surfaced the same observation this session: **the fine-grained PAT is stored in plaintext in the Claude project instructions**, where it is visible to anything that reads that context. The bootstrap's own terms already require rotation "after sharing any chat or this project, and on any suspicion of exposure." Nothing indicates misuse, and the token's blast radius is bounded by design (single repo, Contents read/write only, ≤90-day expiry) — but three unprompted flags is itself the suspicion the term contemplates. **Recommended: rotate at github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens, update the token line in the project instructions, and re-paste.** No repo change is needed and no session action is taken without the user's instruction. **✓ CLOSED 2026-09-12 — the PAT was rotated after the Cowork probe run exposed the v1.1.1 token line in a hosted transcript; BOOTSTRAP v1.2.0 carries the new line and records the rotation date. The flag text below is kept as the record of why.** *(Raised 2026-08-27. **Was still open 2026-09-09** — the token is now in ~27 transcripts and was used successfully from a cloud sandbox by three scheduled runs; the post-mortem in `queue/runs/` recommends revoking it outright once the scheduled tasks are recreated with the repo attached.)*
- **Retrieval-environment constraint — new, and it shapes what can be scheduled.** Three classes of primary source refused this session: **network-policy 403s** at the egress proxy (assets.asics.com, api.alertir.com, mb.cision.com, data.sec.gov, efts.sec.gov), **robots.txt blocks** (release.tdnet.info, sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar), and **fetch-approval gates** that time out unanswered in an unattended run (investors.hexagon.com, s204.q4cdn.com, sec.gov/Archives directory listings, several live-quote pages). Company IR sites that render via JavaScript (Straumann, Zip) return empty shells. **Consequence for the overnight-runner design below: the data-completeness gate is not a theoretical guardrail — it fired twice today**, on Hexagon and Gap, and would have produced two wrong verdicts had it not. Log which hosts work per filer in each memo's Appendix A, as the retrieval doctrine already requires.
- **✓ "Cartara" → Certara (CERT) — CONFIRMED by user 2026-08-18.** Flag closed; the CERT row stands (AVOID, two gates, analysed 2026-08-09).
- **Bed Bath & Beyond** — resolved by evidence, analysed as **BBBY** (AVOID, three gates); **NXH re-key pending** the rename's effectiveness (see Corporate actions).

The 21 Ireland, Portugal and India names of the re-seed block are appended to `queue/DRAFT_QUEUE.md` v7. `2222.SR` stays screened as state-controlled (Tier C). `IHC.AE` (Abu Dhabi ADX) and Masterplast (stale `.BD` quote) are data-access items, not framework blocks. *Was:* 23 names blocked since 2026-08-31 on missing `.NS`/`.BO`, `.IR`, `.LS`, `.SR`, `.AE` and `.BD` keys.

- **What the register's shape argues for (analysis, not a recommendation).** Coverage by exchange suffix across the 317 rows live at the 2026-08-28 snapshot (319 now): US 154 · Germany 34 · Sweden 28 · France 22 · Japan 18 · Switzerland 13 · Netherlands 9 · Poland 6 · **UK 6** · Italy 5 · Denmark 5 · Finland 4 · Hong Kong 3 · **Canada 2** · Taiwan, Israel, Shanghai, Shenzhen, Madrid, Korea, **Australia 1 each** · Norway, Belgium, Austria, Ireland, Portugal, India, Singapore **0**. Germany has 34 rows and the UK 6, against comparable universes of quality industrials — that gap is an artefact of the block order the project happened to run, not a judgment anyone made. The UK, Canada and Australia blocks are where the marginal name is most likely to be both new and analysable on existing machinery.

- **v20 — 2026-09-01** — KSPI v3 committed at register v279. **A user-directed lens change, not a business change: WATCH → AVOID.** The §2·1A financial-institution substitution was set aside and the industrial machinery run instead; Past fails on leverage (L/E 340% FY2025 / 353% 1H26 against the 200% flag, with P6's carve-out unmet because the borrowing is retail deposits funding a loan book, not repurchase funding) and independently on a three-year uninterrupted ROCE decline. **The lens is the whole flip** — no deposit-taker passes the literal leverage test, and the balance-sheet condition v2 relied on still holds. Routed to IDEAS A2, of which this is now the most consequential instance: the first where the two readings give opposite *final verdicts* rather than one gate. **Three method findings.** (1) The user's directed cash-flow check was right to run and did not bear out as suspected — v2's FY2025 FCF of ₸491,098m correctly excluded the Hepsiburada consideration — but it exposed a larger problem: reported CFO embeds the loan-book/deposit gap, so FCF swings ₸1,056bn → ₸486bn → ₸491bn → ₸953bn on trading that did not move that way. TTM FCF is **₸952,971m**, taking MCap/FCF from v2's 18.7× to **9.6×**. Both bases now carried and labelled at every use. (2) **v2's EV/OI "n.m., 5.4×–11.4× spread" was an inconsistency, not uncertainty** — deposits added to EV while their interest cost stayed deducted from the denominator. Paired financing-blind the metric is 6.7×–7.8× and becomes the binding band leg. Worth sweeping any other memo that marked EV/OI n.m. on a deposit funder. (3) **A v2 data gap was a retrieval miss, not a disclosure gap:** Kaspi furnishes two 6-K exhibits within days of each other, and the interim financial-information exhibit carries a full IAS-34 cash-flow statement, the NCI split and the buy-back share count that v2 recorded as unavailable. Standing lesson: for foreign private issuers, check for a second 6-K before recording a disclosure gap. Band ≈$98 → ≈₸39,000/$86; Dist +8% → +21%. All figures computed in tenge — third-party USD/KZT quotes for late August diverge 455–477, so USD uses the ADS-implied cross of 454.89 and distances are FX-invariant. **Re-merged onto live head:** the session base was v18; a parallel 2026-08-28 recovery landed v19 (the 239-name coverage-audit and re-seed blocks) between fetch and PUT, and §7.4a base verification caught it — renumbered v19 → v20 with no work lost on either side. v19 archived → `queue/archive/TODO_v19_2026-08-31.md`.
